These former members are returning to Congress next year

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Even in an election cycle marked by surprises and upsets, Capitol Hill observers will spot some familiar faces (identical twins notwithstanding) among this year’s midterm winners.

At least seven former members won comeback bids Tuesday.

The winners included alumni who dusted themselves off after election losses just two years ago. While other winners haven’t been in office in nearly two decades, their service predating the addition of a women’s restroom in the House (more recent than you may guess).

Here’s how these former members did on election night:

Carl lost a primary to fellow Republican Rep. Barry Moore in 2024 after a new map placed them in the same district. But with Moore running for the Senate in 2026, Carl had an easier path back to his old seat, which was redrawn again this year. He defeated his Democratic opponent Tuesday by 18 points.

It has been nearly two decades since Solis last served in Congress. She left in 2009 to serve as President Barack Obama’s first Labor secretary. Solis was in her sixth term as a Los Angeles county supervisor when she announced a bid for Congress after California voters approved a map that created five Democratic pickup opportunities.

Solis faced limited resistance on her path to victory in the 38th District, especially after Democratic Rep. Linda T. Sánchez opted to run for the redrawn 41st District.

Bean is making her way back to the House after losing her suburban Chicagoland seat in the conservative tea party wave of 2010.

A decade-and-a-half later, she won an eight-way primary for the 8th District seat Democrat Raja Krishnamoorthi vacated for an unsuccessful Senate bid. Winning the nomination all but guaranteed Bean a November victory in the deep-blue seat.

One of the cycle’s biggest comeback stories was in the Buckeye State, where Sherrod Brown was mounting a comeback bid two years after Republican Bernie Moreno had unseated him. Positioning himself as an opponent of data centers, Brown defeated appointed GOP Sen. Jon Husted in a special election for the remainder of Vice President JD Vance’s term. He could take office as early as December but would have to run again in 2028 if he wants another full term.

First elected in 2018, Allred left Congress two years ago after an unsuccessful Senate campaign against Republican incumbent Ted Cruz. After briefly launching a second Senate bid, Allred switched to a redrawn open House seat and won his primary over Rep. Julie Elizabeth Johnson, his successor in the chamber. Allred had no trouble winning his general election Tuesday for a fourth House term.

McAdams served one term in Congress representing a battleground Utah seat, before being unseated by Republican Burgess Owens in 2020. But after a state judge approved a new congressional map that created a heavily Democratic seat anchored in Salt Lake City, McAdams jumped into the race. He comfortably won his primary over more progressive opponents and stomped his way to a second term on Tuesday.

Once a high-ranking member of the House Armed Services Committee, Luria lost her Hampton Roads seat in 2022 to Republican Jen Kiggans.

But a week after Virginia Democrats posted strong showings in the commonwealth’s 2025 off-year elections, Luria launched her House comeback bid and went on to oust Kiggans on Tuesday.

Former House Intelligence Chairman Mike Rogers left the House after the 2014 elections and made his first Senate bid 10 years later. He lost to Democrat Elissa Slotkin but sought the seat of retiring Democratic Sen. Gary Peters the following year.

Rogers tried to take advantage of Democratic divisions between primary winner Abdul El-Sayed and Rep. Haley Stevens, even winning a late endorsement from the congresswoman. But it was not enough to stop El-Sayed on Tuesday.

Republican John E. Sununu served three terms in the House and one in the Senate before losing to Democrat Jeanne Shaheen in 2008. With Shaheen retiring, Sununu attempted a comeback but ended up losing decisively to Democratic Rep. Chris Pappas.

Two years after losing her at-large House seat to Republican Nick Begich, Peltola chose to challenge Republican Sen. Dan Sullivan. The race was rocked by late revelations by The New York Times that top aides to Peltola’s campaign were closely involved in recruiting a second candidate named Dan Sullivan into the race to peel votes from the incumbent. As of Wednesday afternoon, Peltola was clinging to a narrow lead as votes were counted.

Perriello won an upset victory in a Charlottesville-anchored district in 2008 before losing reelection in the tea party wave two years later. He challenged freshman Republican John McGuire for the current version of the seat but was narrowly trailing as of Wednesday afternoon and McGuire has already declared victory.

"Arkane is profitable," say Marvel's Blade developers in fiery new statement, "just not profitable enough for Microsoft"

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Unionised workers reiterate call for a "solid offer" for Arkane Lyon's future

Unionised workers at Dishonored developers Arkane Lyon have published a fresh statement about the progress of negotiations with Microsoft over the studio's potential closure. Aside from calling on Xbox management to make a redundancy offer proportionate to Microsoft's colossal market value, they accuse the parent company of creating the very economic conditions cited as a reason for closing Arkane, and thereby "sabotaging" development of the forthcoming Marvel's Blade.

Shared by French union Le Syndicat des Travailleureuses du Jeu Vidéo, the (slightly shonky) official English translation of the statement begins by insisting that "work on Blade is going fine" and that the game will measure up to previous Arkane Lyon releases, despite the stress of trying to pin down a new owner for the company, after Microsoft announced plans to part ways with Arkane and several other studios back in July. It then takes Microsoft to task for making an "outrageously low" redundancy offer to Arkane's workers, and further accuses the Xbox company of essentially setting Arkane up to fail by imposing a hiring freeze and depriving the studio of resources.

"Microsoft chose to justify the closure on economic reasons," the workers write. "But Microsoft is actually why those economic reasons even exist." It asserts that Microsoft froze hiring "for 19 months, which artificially caused delay and additional cost", only to then use those delays and extra costs "as a reason to close down the studio". It also claims that Microsoft have "systematically" deprived Arkane of cash, by means of corporate mechanisms the statement doesn't make wholly clear. "Even this closure itself is done against any financial logic, as closing Arkane today would probably be more expensive than finishing the game," the statement adds, without going into detail.

"The truth is that Microsoft based its decision only on stone cold and inhuman logic, in which our lives, projects and dreams are voided, so we can better serve Microsoft’s short term financial goals," the workers continue. They accuse Microsoft of "the brutal application of a never-ending search for maximum profit optimization", citing years of mass layoffs across the Xbox company together with the recent voluntary buyout offering. "Arkane is profitable," they add. "Just not profitable enough for Microsoft."

The workers want a "solid offer" from Microsoft regarding the studio's post-Xbox future, declaring that "when the company who decided that Arkane has to shut down is, according to many sources the most profitable in the world, with a net profit of more than 133 Billions of dollars in the past years, it would be the bare minimum to ensure that the redundancy plan offered by Microsoft is on [par] with their means."

If Arkane have to close, the unionised workers call on Microsoft "to release funds commensurate with Microsoft’s status as a corporation, to make sure that the workers are compensated in line with the loss suffered". They'd like a redundancy payment of €200,000, two years of "redeployment support", a package of "shared resources for training and studio creation", including a residual bonus, and "compensation up to the redundancy payment for independent workers’ contract breach", to safeguard the livelihoods of freelancers and contractors.

The warring parties at Xbox and Arkane have until 13th December this year to figure out a deal. Reportedly, Ubisoft are among those looking to buy Arkane - a source of some concern, given that Ubisoft have also recently closed a number of studios and laid many people off. I'm not sure I'd be waving my wallet around if I were using my other hand to sign pink slips.

10 Best Chain Italian Subs, According To Customer Reviews

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The Italian sub is its own category of cuisine. Born of the ingenuity of Italian immigrants, this centuries-old sandwich balances the highest forms of culinary technique with the simplest combination of ingredients. Expertly cured meats, creamy provolone cheese, crisp vegetables, and the right formula of oil and vinegar — all layered on a crusty roll, hoagie, or baguette — meld into a perfectly harmonious bite that's simultaneously salty, tangy, punchy, and fresh. Within this basic framework is room for limitless personalization. Some shops adorn their Italian sandwich with mayonnaise or hot relish. Another might add prosciutto or smoked ham or a stack of vinegary banana peppers. One may even toast the thing.

That's why the Italian sub is such a fun food to explore. No two are identical — even if they use exactly the same ingredients. Obviously, a sandwich with so much history is an easy target for dissection and judgment. Customers have their favorites, especially when it comes to what's offered at popular chain restaurants. And they're not afraid to share their feelings. For this article, I looked through hundreds of reviews, comments, and posts to find the Italian sub sandwiches with the highest customer ratings and feedback. The chains on this list range from dedicated sandwich spots to supermarkets to gas–station chains, but they all put together a great Italian sub.

With higher-quality ingredients, sliced-to-order meats, and lettuce shredded so thin it defies Newtonian mechanics, you could argue Jersey Mike's has successfully redefined the fast-casual sub. But a sandwich shop — especially one with the word "Jersey" in its name — is really only as good as its Italian sub. And in this department, Mike's Original Italian (AKA "The #13") delivers. It's a classic "Jersey-style" Italian sandwich, filled with provolone, ham, prosciuttini, capicola, salami, and pepperoni, creating a symphony of sweet, spicy, and savory meats.

"I went to Jersey Mike's for the first time and had one of the best subs I've had in a long time – #13 Italian sub done Mike's way," starts one Reddit thread. "13, Mike's way, add relish is the best Italian sandwich I have had since buying them from Italian bakeries as a kid." noted another commenter. The overall consensus is that Jersey Mike's takes pride in its product, and that getting the sandwich "Mike's Way," with a topping of tomato, onion, olive oil, red wine vinegar, oregano, and salt, elevates the experience to what you may find at a local Italian deli. It seems the community has spoken: Jersey Mike's is the best.

With over 2,500 locations across the country, Jimmy John's is one of the largest sandwich chains in the U.S., and while not all its sandwiches receive universal praise, one exception stands out: the JJ's Italian Night Club. And just like its major competitor, Jersey Mike's, Jimmy John's doesn't reinvent the wheel with its Italian sandwich. It's a classic combination of meat (salami, capicola, ham), provolone cheese, onion, lettuce, tomato, mayo, oil, vinegar, and seasonings, all served on freshly baked French bread.

Customers love the bread at Jimmy John's — just not its appearance. "Their bread looks plain AF, but it really do hit," said one Redditor. "That bread looks sad, but the Italian night club with hot peppers is the only sandwich that gets my money there," mentioned another. To deal with this boring bread, regulars recommend toasting it to achieve a light-brown crust and accentuate its yeasty aroma. The aforementioned hot peppers are another game changer, adding a tart, vinegary bite and lingering heat that cuts through the otherwise rich and meaty sandwich. One insider suggests that, when ordering the hot peppers, ask for extra.

While Jersey Mike's and Jimmy John's have thousands of locations scattered throughout the country, there are a handful of smaller regional chains serving quality, thoughtfully assembled sub sandwiches. Penn Station is one of them. With over 300 restaurants across the South and Midwest, Penn Station is winning over the hearts (and stomachs) of its customers with a concentrated menu of subs, wraps, hand-crafted sandwiches, and seasonal specials. As with most sub chains, its Classic Grilled Italian is getting the most attention. Along with the typical ingredient lineup, Penn Station uses smoked ham and banana peppers, minor tweaks that add a little electricity to its signature sandwich.

On Reddit, the sandwich receives gushing adoration from regular customers. "Penn Station's Italian sub has a hold on me," starts one thread. A lot of the love has to do with the mayonnaise, which one person described as "otherworldly." But what makes up the bulk of every sub sandwich is its bread, and here, again, Penn Station doesn't falter, with several customers noting its flavor and texture as a main contributor to the chain's overall success.

DiBella's began as a neighborhood grocery store in upstate New York around a hundred years ago. A lot has changed in the centuries since, just not the quality of its sandwiches, which have remained undeniably delicious for decades. Now, I know that we've talked a lot about bread already, but DiBella's might produce the best loaf of the bunch. The dough is aged for a minimum of 16 hours, scored by hand, and baked fresh two times a day to ensure customers never bite into something even remotely stale, which is why one enthusiastic customer called it the GOAT.

Online, people are impressed that the chain can execute mom-and-pop level taste and freshness (and an impeccable meatball sub). "DiBella's is amazing...It's hard to believe it's a chain," noted a fan. But how's the sandwich itself? Well, with a name like DiBella's, you know that its signature sandwich, aptly named "The Godfather" delivers everything you could want in a good Italian sub. All in all, it's a very solid sandwich that needs to be on your radar.

Cheba Hut's branding reads like something from a college dorm room, but its food quality is anything but juvenile or half-baked. Across the board, Cheba Hut does solid sandwiches, but its La Canna has emerged as a fan favorite, with one thread titled "My New Favorite Sub," getting over 100 upvotes on Reddit.

The La Canna distinguishes itself from other Italian subs by using prosciutto in place of the more common capicola, along with Genoa salami and smoked ham. Equally important is the ingredient balance, which pairs crisp vegetables, meat, and cheese with a tangy "shake & house" dressing. "Can't beat a La Canna on garlic herb," said one Redditor. But what makes Cheba Hut so special is its general culture of sandwich lawlessness. There are few rules here; you can get your sandwiches with alfalfa sprouts or cream cheese, on garlic bread, or served alongside a bowl of French onion soup for dipping.

Potbelly doesn't get the respect it deserves. While most of the chains on this list specialize in sandwiches, Potbelly does it all: wraps, salads, soups, subs, chili, milkshakes, mac and cheese, and one of the best club sandwiches around. But with so much variety, it's easy to be fooled into thinking it's a "jack of all trades, master of none" scenario, but this isn't the case at all.

But the Italian sandwich, although slightly more conservative than its other sandwich offering, is still tasty and reliable. Unlike other chains, Potbelly offers four — not three — heavy-hitting meats, including salami, old-world capicola, pepperoni, and mortadella — a detail that only the most observant sandwich snobs appreciate. "Their Italian is the best chain sub shop Italian out there. Nowhere else has mortadella," noted a Redditor. Others appreciate the sandwich, as well, with one person saying it's their "top choice for a commercial chain place." But what really sets this sandwich apart from the others is the giardiniera, a quintessential Chicago topping of hot pickled peppers, carrots, celery, and other veggies. The condiment is so desirable that Potbelly even sells it by the jar.

Unlike some of the other sandwich chains on this list, PrimoHoagies specializes in Italian subs. And that distinction, especially for a Philadelphia-based operation, leaves little room for error. PrimoHoagies is so committed to the Italian sandwich that it offers not one, not two, but five different variations, each with a different formula of sweet, spicy, and sharp meats and cheeses. Online, the sandwiches at PrimoHoagie's are both widely celebrated and the source of debate, specifically for the quantity of meat packed between its bread.

But many customers think this is a good problem and actually appreciate the quantity and value. In many ways, this is the most authentic sandwich of the bunch. It doesn't come with bells and whistles, just a short list of quality ingredients layered on an "award-winning seeded bread." And if you really have an issue with the quantity of cold cuts, just ask the sandwich makers for some extra veggies.

No one thinks twice about grabbing groceries at a supermarket, but for whatever reason, when it comes to prepared foods — especially sandwiches — shoppers are a little more hesitant. But when you really think about it, the supermarket, with its endless inventory of fresh vegetables, breads, meats, and cheeses, is the ideal place to grab a sub. Publix, a chain of grocery stores popular in Florida and the Southern United States, has made a name for itself in the sandwich department. Whereas other supermarkets haphazardly throw their sandwiches together, Publix constructs its subs with care and attention.

Online, customers voice their appreciation. "There are few things better in life than a Pub Sub," mentioned a Redditor. And despite being a regional supermarket chain, Publix has legions of followers across the country who will make a special trip whenever they're nearby. The supermarket's Italian sub, which comes with the classic trio of meats and provolone, isn't revolutionary, but what sets the sandwich apart is the quality of the vegetables and freshly-baked Italian Five Grain sub roll. Add some oil and vinegar for a perfectly balanced bite.

Wawa has a cult following, and for good reason. Its to-go meals, subs, sides, and secret off-menu items are terrific, even compared to fast-casual chains dedicated specifically to the craft of sandwich making. If you're unfamiliar with Wawa, you can order via a handy touchpad, where you can order sandwiches as-is or customize them to your preferences. If you suffer from choice paralysis, its predesigned subs are a terrific option. One that constantly scores high marks is its Italian sub.

In a ranking of all the Wawa subs (or hoagies if you're from Philadelphia), The Takeout declared the Italian the best option, citing its flavorful collection of meats and deft blend of salty, spicy, and fresh ingredients. This is far from a fluke. One Reddit thread dedicated to Wawa's Italian Sub has over 400 upvotes, with users describing their specific orders in the comments. Some people go standard, but many regulars like to add extra meat and cheese, jalapeños, oil and vinegar, or garlic aioli. But it's the entire Wawa experience that has won over the hearts of its customers. You can fill up your gas tank, grab a quick sandwich, and be back on the road in just a few minutes.

Another gas station moonlighting as a fast-casual stop for quality grub, QuickChek isn't so different from Wawa. And the same goes for the quality of its subs. Both chains offer a similar digital ordering platform where customers can choose every aspect of their sandwich, from the toppings to the bread. Sub sandwiches shouldn't leave you hungry, and QuickChek is a bang for your buck. But don't take our word for it. In 2021, QuickChek was named the "Best Value" for Italian subs in New Jersey, according to New Jersey Monthly.

And when you see some of its sandwiches, you'll know why. One of its Signature Subs, the Giant Italian, lives up to its name: a mammoth foot-long sub loaded with ham, salami, pepperoni, fresh mozzarella, tomato, onion, lettuce, garlic aioli, and a dynamite cherry pepper relish. On Facebook, regulars agree that for the price, speed, and accessibility, it's hard to beat a QuickChek sandwich. It's all love for this affordable convenience chain.

I used Reddit, Facebook, roundups, and other social media channels to identify the best Italian subs from popular chains. Some were obvious, but others surprised me. I kept it to chains with more than 50 locations.

If a supermarket or gas station/QSR hybrid reportedly puts together consistently good subs, it made the list. You'll notice a couple of the big names were excluded, and that's due to the fact that it seems that the quality has slipped so low that the general consensus amongst consumers, especially ones knowledgeable/passionate enough to discuss sandwiches on message boards, was unfavorable.

Qatar Launches Two South American Destinations

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Qatar Airways added two South American destinations on Wednesday, becoming the first Gulf carrier to serve Venezuela.

The new service flies from Doha to Bogotá, Colombia, then continues to Caracas, Venezuela, before returning nonstop to Doha. Qatar Airways will operate the route on Wednesdays and Sundays using a Boeing 777-200LR.

Flights are scheduled to leave Doha at 7:35 a.m. and reach Bogotá at 4:25 p.m. After a 90-minute stop, the aircraft continues to Caracas, arriving at 9 p.m. The return flight leaves Caracas at 11:30 p.m. and reaches Doha at 8:35 p.m. the following day. All times are local.

The airline originally planned to start flying the route in July but postponed the launch following an earthquake in Venezuela, according to Aviation Week.

Qatar Airways is also expanding its codeshare with LATAM to include domestic flights from Bogotá. The agreement will put Qatar’s code on LATAM flights to destinations including Medellín, Cartagena and Cali.

The additions bring Qatar Airways’ network in the Americas to 17 destinations. The carrier began serving South America in 2010 with flights to São Paulo.

Bitcoin and Ethereum ETFs shed $1.8B – Why investors are pulling back

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The Spot Bitcoin [BTC] ETFs witnessed a sustained wave of outflows between the 30th of October and the 3rd of November, recording approximately $1.16 billion in cumulative net outflows in just three trading sessions.

According to Farside Investors data, on the 30th of October, the ETFs recorded their largest single-day outflow of the period, worth $643.2 million.

In fact, the negative momentum continued on the 2nd of November, when BTC ETFs recorded another $398.6 million in net outflows. On the 3rd of November, however, the outflow momentum eased considerably, with total withdrawals declining to $115.6 million.

In these three days, BlackRock’s IBIT alone accounted for $734 million in outflows, making it the biggest contributor to the negative trend.

On the other hand, spot Ethereum [ETH] ETFs continued to experience persistent capital outflows for seven consecutive days from the 26th of October to the 3rd of November 2026. Overall, Ethereum spot ETFs recorded approximately $656.4 million in cumulative net outflows.

Remarking on the same, Bloomberg analyst James Seyffart said,

Things were looking pretty good into the end of September but a chunk of that reversed course in October.

He further added that the average cost basis of ETF holdings stood at $3,182, compared to ETH’s market price of approximately $2,341, which left investors with aggregate unrealized losses of nearly $3.47 billion.

Meanwhile, on the altcoin front, XRP ETFs witnessed a $529.51K net outflow on the 2nd of November.

Whereas, Binance [BNB] ETFs recorded net outflows of $271.01K on 3rd November. Hyperliquid [HYPE] spot ETFs, however, stood out with positive momentum, recording $605.08K in net inflows on the same day, following substantial outflows of $2.86 million a day earlier.

In contrast, Solana [SOL] spot ETFs recorded zero flows on both days.

All this happened amidst a tough macro backdrop, where the U.S. Federal Reserve’s October decision to maintain interest rates at 3.75%–4.00% ignited concerns about persistent inflation.

Meanwhile, the 3rd November U.S. midterm elections added to market caution, while a modest recovery in the U.S. dollar further pressured dollar-denominated assets.

Topping all of this was the Binance security breach that triggered fresh anxiety across the cryptocurrency market, potentially accelerating institutional withdrawals.

Brighton and Liverpool made unfashionable tactical pivots at the weekend that could be the start of a new trend

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation plus sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Play the viral 442GOALS game and build an XI of Premier League legends!

Fantastic football content straight to your inbox! From the latest transfer news, quizzes, videos, features and interviews with the biggest names in the game, plus lots more.

Sign up to our FREE live football newsletter, tracking all of the biggest games available to watch on the device of your choice. Never miss a kick-off!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

If these two clubs are doing it, it's worth investigating...

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Crossing isn’t exactly a dying art, but it has certainly become rarer to see the more ambitious, attack-minded teams swing crosses into the box from open play.

It just isn’t de rigueur to try something so low-percentage, something so unfashionably anti-aesthetic.

But two of the most attacking clubs (with two of the most modern, zeitgeisty tacticians) pivoted to crossing at the weekend, so it is worth considering whether this is the beginning of a new trend that will catch on.

Fashion, after all, plays an outsized role in tactical theory in the Premier League, and if Brighton and Liverpool are doing it, then it must be Smart and Good.

Liverpool’s 12 open-play crosses in the first half on Sunday were the most any team have managed against Arsenal since November 2020. By the 42nd minute, when Alex Mac Allister should have scored with a header from what was their 19th cross (open or set-piece) of the match, Liverpool had almost quadrupled the five crosses they made in the whole game against Brighton the weekend before.

Brighton weren’t as consistent in their 2-0 victory over Manchester City, but there was a clear tactical plan to switch the play with long diagonals into the (very wide) wingers as often as possible. Fabian Hurzeler appeared to see City’s not-actually-full-backs as the weak spot, and it worked, leading directly to the winning goal.

Liverpool and Brighton had different reasons for their strategies, although the end result was the same: both knew that by getting balls into the box they could sew chaos. Brighton’s winner came via a deflected half-clearance, while Arsenal’s defence regularly made first contact but, now on the back foot, were penned in by the loose second ball (at their most dishevelled, Jurrien Timber pulled on Alexander Isak’s shirt).

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

As Iraola put it: “We’ve put them under pressure, we’ve put them inside the box, we’ve put them there.”

Arsenal, uncomfortable, were pushed back into the box; were 'put there'. It is a simple, albeit unfashionable, idea that others may choose to follow.

‘Enough Phone’ is an Android phone roughly the size of iPhone 13 Mini, launching in 2027

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Everyone says they want small Android phones back, and 2027 might be your chance to actually buy one. The “Enough Phone” has a mere 5.3-inch display, decent specs, and a price of $599 and less.

Launching in May 2027, the “Enough Phone” is a 5.3-inch Android phone that simply does want to be “enough.”

The company says the 5.3-inch LCD display comes in at 1520×720 and that the phone will be powered by a MediaTek Dimensity 7500 chipset, 8GB of RAM, and offers 128GB of storage. It’ll come with either “stock Android” or OdysseyOS, an Android skin used by SLEKE and designed to be minimal and act like a modern dumbphone. SLEKE currently sells repurposed Pixel phones running this software.

Android 17 will run out of the box with updates through 2032.

“Enough Phone” measures in at 132.8 x 63.8 x 12mm and will have a battery between 3,500 and 4,000 mAh which will be user-replaceable. While it’s thicker, the physical size is roughly the same as iPhone 13 Mini.

Rounding out the package would be a fingerprint sensor in the power button, a single 50MP (Sony IMX766) camera on the back, and a 32MP selfie camera up front.

The company says that these specs are “mostly final” and that pricing is planned for $599.

“Enough Phone” is funding on Kickstarter with $399, $449, and $499 early bird reservation tiers ahead of the May 2027 shipping estimate.

Inaugural Walker Design Triennial features flesh-like speakers and post-apocalyptic scenes

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A set of speakers with flesh-like exteriors was among the conceptual design work shown at the first Walker Design Triennial in Minneapolis, USA, which curator Joseph Grima said reflected on the meaning of design in a world fixated on optimisation.

Filling a series of galleries at the Walker Art Center in Minneapolis, Minnesota, the exhibition was called Beyond Materialism and featured numerous examples of conceptual design.

The pieces ranged from the historical works such as Italian radical designer Alessandro Mendini’s 1970s Lassù – paired with depictions of the performance of the chair on fire – to an international set of design studios representing the field today.

Grima, who has served as a curator and educator for several high-profile institutions including Design Academy Eindhoven and international exhibition series Alcova, said the show was meant to challenge mainstream conceptions of design.

“We live in an age that is conditioned to think that anything and everything can be designed, and often what is that everything can be optimised,” said Grima.

“And it’s precisely this association between design and optimisation that this exhibition intends to question.”

With objects of various scales on display, the Walker Design Triennial featured a room with large-scale conceptual installations. Many of the participants were international, with a cohort of contemporary US designers, including Germane Barnes and Theaster Gates.

Terracotta plinths were included as part of the exhibition design, supporting many of the small and large objects.

Some of the pieces were commissioned directly for the event, mainly in the large-scale section.

Among these was Spanish designer Lucas Muñoz Muñoz’s All Ears, a set of latex-covered speakers that were programmed to play sounds recorded in the months before in Minneapolis. A sleek soundboard allowed users to play the tracks, with multiple tracks routed through the amorphous, fleshy speakers all at once to cacophonic effect.

Muñoz told Dezeen that the installation was designed to be a “microphone” for the city itself and “make visible the act of listening”.

The broadcast from the soundboard was also projected out of the gallery as a radio sequence.

Also commissioned for the event was a work by Vessel, featuring two massive rolls of fabric spooled over a kiosk. The fabric was based on Vessel cofounder Navine G Dossos’s paintings of her and her partner James Bridle’s self-built home.

Also in the large-scale room was a series of post-apocalyptic appliances made from found objects by Brussels-based Espace Aygo, derived from the studio’s 2022 transformation of a derelict apartment building into a home and art space.

The themes of fleshy objects, post-apocalypticism, and reuse continued into the rooms with smaller objects.

Here, London-based studio Superflux showcased objects from its Relics of Abundance series, showing objects created by a future civilisation that may someday use our objects today in a time of scarcity, including a chair made from straw and rebar.

Austro-Canadian designer Christoph Wimmer Ruelland created a series of furniture pieces with found objects that are extraordinarily colour-matched, while Korean designer Jonghoo Jeong utilised jerrycans to create a fountain, a reference to his past practice of dumping the objects in his process.

Athens-based studio Astronauts showcased inflated metal works informed by the autobody shops, creating love seats, chairs and even a seesaw that are iridescent while still familiar.

Italian-German designer Maximilian Marchesani created a structural piece made of smartphone glass that refracts metal-coated leaves as well as a chandelier that combined LEDs with branches. South Korean studio Wknd Lab created a series of stools made from discarded shells.

Dozens of other designers took part in the installation, including Moon Seop Seo, Studio Ker, Objects of Common Interest, Stef Fusani, Kevin Quale and Kostas Lambridis, representing a who’s who of avant-garde design, refracted through the lens of Grima and the event’s artistic director, Asli Altay.

It was an “opportunity to stop, take stock for a moment, and reflect on what design is today,” said Grima.

Other recent design showcases include a show of wood furniture by Armadillo and furniture made from fragments of New York buildings by Rafael Prieto.

Beyond Materialism is on show at the Walker Art Center from 17 October 2026 to 14 February 2027. Find out more about design and architecture exhibitions, talks and fairs at Dezeen Events Guide.

'A Tired Parent Mode': The Witcher 3 Dev Explains the Point of Remaster's Accessibility Options

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

One of the biggest additions to The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt Remastered is a suite of accessibility options, letting players tweak various aspects of the game in order to tone down its mechanical friction.

When used in conjunction with the game's easier difficulty setting, you can transform Geralt's adventure into a very laid back, story-focused experience.

However, some people view these kinds of accessibility features as little more than cheats under a modern name — but that's far from the intention, according to Remastered's director of player experience Karolina Niewęgłowska.

In a really interesting developer commentary post on CD Projekt Red's official forums, Niewęgłowska provides details on the thinking behind Remastered's accessibility suite — which is dubbed 'Bard's Ballad' as a preset.

"Bard’s Ballad is for those having difficulty concentrating, including those with ADHD, or those experiencing cognitive fatigue, for example due to conditions like depression," she explains.

"But it’s also for folks who simply feel tired or overwhelmed in this busy world. A colleague to whom I described Bard’s Ballad said 'so… a tired parent mode'. Very much so. But it is for anyone who wants to use it, to play in a way that fits their needs best."

In truth, I've never really thought about accessibility options from this kind of perspective. Sometimes players are just knackered, and they just want to enjoy a bit of escapism without having to commit to a challenge.

"If you only have an hour at the end of your busy day, and have little energy left to manage and organise things, it may be the opposite of what you need. And Bard’s Ballad is addressing that," the director continues.

Niewęgłowska also brings up the smaller toggles, and how they can help players who might be struggling with OCD-like impulses — which is fascinating in itself, given how many open world games push you to visit every little map marker and collect every possible resource.

"There are also settings under Bard’s Ballad to turn off showing herbs and corpses on the minimap. Those are normally helpful. But for some people the dots on the minimap are a distraction, calling 'hey, I’m here, do something about me!', or induce uncertainty or fear of missing out: 'do I have to collect them all?'"

"No, you don’t have to, especially if your main motivation is to experience the story and the world. If having fewer signals on the screen can help you enjoy the game in peace and focus, the toggles are there for you."

She concludes: "Another major change that deserves a highlight is the rise of cognitive overload in the modern world. The digital world constantly bombards us with stimuli, and each year there is more. Notifications, ads, feeds, reminders. Never before in history have humans been exposed to as much information daily. And if we consider this context, ways to manage cognitive load become more useful than ever."

I think Niewęgłowska's post ends up being really insightful, so I thought it was worth sharing here. The Witcher 3's over a decade old now, and I reckon anything that can help as many people as possible enjoy such a landmark game deserves praise.

Have you made use of Remastered's accessibility options? Have you struggled with any of the issues that Niewęgłowska brings up? Take it easy in the comments section below.

The Best-Rated Game Of 2026 Has Just Changed, But Xbox Still Has Two Titles In The Top 10

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The news cycle has undoubtedly been dominated with The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake today, and for good reason - it's become the best-rated game of 2026 according to Metacritic with a whopping 96 average rating.

That means the top 10 has some shuffling to do, but even with Zelda taking top spot (and even if GTA 6 surpasses it in November), there are still two Xbox first-party releases that are almost certainly locked into the top 10 for 2026.

The first of these is Forza Horizon 6, which now sits in seventh position across all platforms with its 90 average rating, serving as the best full game that Xbox has released this year according to Metacritic.

The other is DOOM: The Dark Ages - Revelations, which might be an expansion to an existing title, but is still counted by the platform (and to be fair, basically serves as an extra game anyway) and sits in sixth place with a 91 rating.

Go down a bit further, and Gears of War: E-Day is the next highest-rated Xbox first-party game with an average rating of 87, followed by Modern Warfare 4 at 85, Diablo IV: Lord of Hatred at 84 and Halo: Campaign Evolved at 81.

In a nutshell, it's been a pretty incredible year for video games! Don't forget that Sektori, The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt - Remastered and Mina The Hollower have all released on Xbox this year as well, while Xbox still has another massive first-party title dropping tomorrow in the form of World of Warcraft: Forever - albeit only on PC.

Ford Says Even the Fathom EV’s Fanciest Trims Will Stay Under $40K

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Ford says its top-spec Fathom EV truck will still start well below the average cost of a new car.

Skeptics of the Ford Fathom EV pickup have questioned Ford’s ability to provide a sub-$30,000 truck since its introduction last year, but Andrew Frick, president of Ford’s Model e division, told the Detroit Free Press that even the top trim will remain below $40,000.

After taking a prototype Fathom for a test drive last month, Ford CEO Jim Farley was reportedly blown away by the small truck’s handling and performance, calling it a “breakthrough product.” He also said the Fathom is on track to hit the market toward the end of 2027, though the team will need to keep developing at a rapid pace to get it out on time and within budget.

See all results for new 2028 Ford Fathom for sale near 14086

Ford announced the Fathom’s starting price in August. At $29,945, the pickup truck offers an affordable way to enter the EV world, and it’s the first to ride on Ford’s Universal Electric Vehicle (UEV) platform. The Fathom is already confirmed to be equipped with a touchscreen, built-in Apple Maps, Apple CarPlay, Android Auto, and Blue Cruise hands-free driving capabilities.

The UEV platform operates on a 400-volt electrical architecture, so charging won’t be as quick as vehicles that use an 800-volt system, but Ford says the trade-off for a lighter, more affordable vehicle is worth it. It’ll also use a 48-volt system instead of the traditional 12-volt system used in most vehicles; the higher voltage is intended to increase power and fuel efficiency. Farley revealed this week that the platform will break up the electrical components into five distinct zones to simplify production processes and reduce costs.

The Fathom’s starting price puts it on the more affordable end of the entire car market, including gas-powered and hybrid vehicles, but higher trim levels often bring a significantly higher price to a new car. Ford, however, is reportedly aiming to keep even the highest Fathom trim below $40,000. It’s unclear what will be included in the base model versus the higher specs, but top trims will likely have a longer range, among other upgrades.

The Fathom’s pre-order list is expected to open in the first quarter of 2027, and the truck is slated to go on sale in the last quarter of the year. Pricing beyond the Fathom’s entry point hasn’t yet been announced, but it seems even the highest trim will remain relatively affordable.

See all results for new 2028 Ford Fathom for sale near 14086

Huge Crowds Trek to Everest Base Camp

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The image of an endless line of trekkers heading to Everest Base Camp this week seemed like deja vu. Why are conga lines similar to the infamous ones filmed every May on Everest’s Hillary Step, now repeating at the base of the mountain? We asked the local trekking guide who shot the video.

Ngima Sherpa of Osprey Adventure explained that this is not the norm, but the delay caused by Cyclone Anwar, which hit Nepal last weekend, has created a huge backlog.

“Even though there are always a lot of trekkers heading to Everest Base Camp, this kind of traffic is not usual,” Ngima Sherpa explained. “This video was shot on November 1, after three days of continuous snow and rain…During that time, all the trekkers were stuck in Dingboche, which is two trekking stages away from Everest Base Camp.”

When the weather finally cleared, all those trekkers set off for EBC at the same time, causing heavy traffic on the route from Dingboche to Gorakshep.

The recent cyclone is also the reason why the surroundings are so heavily covered in snow. Usually, the entire trail to Everest Base Camp is dry.

The fresh snow on Nepal’s peaks is having consequences for those planning to summit trekking peaks. Conditions on otherwise straightforward snow slopes have become problematic, as climbers sink into deep, heavy snow.

Satori Adventures had to retreat after swimming in snow on the flanks of Mera Peak, in the Khumbu. “Four to five feet of fresh snow made it impossible to move forward above high camp,” leader Furba Sherpa reported.

Ramesh Gurung noted today that he has retreated from an attempt on 6,119m Lobuche East, another popular Khumbu 6000’er. However, Gurung said he will try again with another group soon.

Conditions will change only as the snow transforms under the dimming sun and freezes in the approaching winter temperatures.

On Ama Dablam, Sherpa climbers struggled to reopen the route to Camp 1 on Tuesday but managed to complete it. The route above Camp 1 follows a steep, mixed ridge with less accumulated snow. Several teams are now on their summit rotations and expect to top out in the next few days.

Tenzing Sherpa of Gokyo Namaste Lodge has shared some useful information for trekkers who plan to cross the huge Ngozumpa Glacier between Gokyo and Dragnag. The recent snowfall makes the usual route less safe, and he recommends following local people along an easier route between Longbanga Tso (1st Lake) and Taujung Tso (2nd Lake). See Tenzing’s sketch of the route:

Alternative route between Gokyo Lakes, suggested by Gokyo Tenzing Sherpa.

Tenzing also noted that the route from Thame to Gokyo is open, as the Gokyo Lodge Association cleared and reopened the trail from the Gokyo side yesterday. However, conditions on the glacier may change quickly.

The route across Gokyo is one of the most popular side trips in the Khumbu, for those wanting to enrich their Everest Base Camp trek. The area features stunning scenery, with high mountain lakes, glaciers, and a view of the formidable South Face of Cho Oyu.

Prior to that, Angela Benavides spent time at/worked at a number of local and international media. She is also experienced in outdoor-sport consultancy for sponsoring corporations, press manager and communication executive, and a published author.

XRP Ledger Activates New Amendment to Strengthen Network

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The XRP Ledger (XRPL) network recently activated a new amendment that helps secure the network by resolving potential security weaknesses and making some existing functionalities more robust and reliable.

This latest amendment, called fixCleanup3_4_0, was activated on Nov. 4, bringing a series of technical updates that include fixes for token issuance, automated market makers (AMMs), lending functionalities, and other parts of the XRP Ledger network.

A fix for a potential security weak point related to token issuance on the XRP Ledger network is a critical aspect of the latest amendment.

As explained in the official documentation of the XRP Ledger network, this update addresses a potential weakness related to delegated permissions that could allow some accounts to issue tokens when they are only allowed to destroy them.

On the XRP Ledger, token issuers can grant permissions to other accounts to perform certain tasks. For example, a stablecoin issuer could delegate permission to burn tokens to another account when customers want to redeem them.

With the latest amendment, the XRP Ledger network addresses this security weakness by preventing a zero-balance, burn-only delegated payment from resulting in the issuance of new tokens.

On top of addressing token issuance permissions, there's more to the fixCleanup3_4_0 amendment.

As explained in the official XRP Ledger network documentation, the fixCleanup3_4_0 update brings several fixes to decentralized trading, asset management, and transaction processing.

For automated market makers (AMMs), this latest update prevents some invalid auction bids and makes some improvements to how liquidity withdrawals and token clawbacks work.

It also brings some fixes for potential unexpected calculation errors or rounding issues that could occur in some cases.

These changes are supposed to make things more predictable when it comes to liquidity pools and avoid potential failures that could happen in some cases.

Another significant aspect of these changes concerns Single Asset Vaults and the proposed native lending infrastructure on the XRP Ledger.

As explained, this amendment brings some improvements to how rounding is handled when assets enter or exit vaults.

The idea is to ensure that transactions do not result in more or fewer assets being transferred than they should.

Atmospheric AI Secures $10M Seed for Procurement Modeling Push

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Atmospheric AI announced on November 5, 2026, that it had raised $10 million in seed funding led by DBL Partners to expand its computational models of raw-material supply chains. DBL Partners published its portfolio page on November 4, 2026. The startup plans to move beyond plastics into chemicals, paper and metals, while DBL’s Vinnie Caruso joins its board.

The funding will also deepen collaboration with an initial group of around 100 manufacturers. That expansion connects the technical work to a commercial push: Dr Torsten Lichtenau is joining as founding chief commercial officer after 22 years at Bain & Company, where he was a senior partner and global head of carbon transition.

Atmospheric AI’s approach begins with the physical processes used to make materials, rather than depending on suppliers to disclose internal operating data. Its models incorporate chemical engineering, plant economics and industrial processes, including relationships among feedstocks, energy, production costs and emissions. The connected system covers oil fields and pipelines, refineries, chemical plants, terminals and shipping routes.

Atmospheric AI says it runs around 50,000 facility models and, in one test, reproduced a producer’s published carbon footprint to within 4.4% without using that producer’s own data. The models are intended to estimate what a material should cost, assess whether a supplier’s price increase is justified, identify other plants that could supply it and examine how a disrupted route could affect cost, carbon or resilience.

The company’s product description presents the platform as a dynamic digital twin of a raw-material value chain, from its origin to the factory gate. Facility-specific cost, carbon and risk information supports should-cost analysis for individual suppliers, supplier-specific Scope 3 emissions metrics and lifecycle-assessment integration. Multi-tier mapping, proactive alerts and sourcing recommendations are intended to bring disruption and negotiation decisions into the same workflow.

Haleon is one manufacturer already working with Atmospheric AI. Chris Low, Haleon’s head of sustainability procurement, said the companies have collaborated for almost two years on resin sourcing. His team now begins with a material’s should-cost, carbon footprint and supply-chain risks rather than relying only on the supplier’s quote, connecting the modeling work directly to a buying decision.

In a customer example described by Atmospheric AI, a manufacturer faced a proposed price increase of $730 per tonne during the Hormuz disruption. The startup’s team calculated that the underlying increase in manufacturing costs was approximately $120 per tonne. That analysis gave the sourcing team a basis for discussing what had changed in production economics instead of simply accepting the proposal or disputing it without evidence.

Co-founder and CEO Roee Goldberg described the long-term goal as a computational model of the raw-material economy: manufacturers would understand the plants and supply networks behind what they buy, with chemical engineering providing the foundation and AI enabling the work at a much larger scale.

The immediate test set by the round is breadth: whether a facility-based approach already applied to resin sourcing can support procurement decisions across additional material categories. Atmospheric AI is pairing that expansion with new commercial leadership and deeper manufacturer collaboration, making the move beyond plastics central to what the seed funding is meant to enable.

Progressives and moderates rejoice: 6 takeaways from the midterms

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Democrats were savoring their midterm election rout Wednesday after decisively flipping both the House and Senate.

“Last night was beyond even our largest expectations of what could happen,” Arizona Rep. Greg Stanton said on a press call.

Hakeem Jeffries, the top House Democrat in line to become the first Black speaker, predicted Democrats would hold at least 255 seats next year, which would be the largest majority for either party since Democrats won 257 seats in 2008.

In the Senate, the Democratic Caucus was poised to hold at least 54 seats after seismic red-state gains, with two key races too close to call.

“What we have now seen is a generational remake of the Senate map that is going to change America not only now but on into the future,” Charles E. Schumer, the chamber’s top Democrat, said Wednesday.

Here are six takeaways from the midterm elections:

Democratic gains were so sweeping that both ideological wings of the party had plenty to celebrate. The moderate Blue Dogs more than doubled their current membership, with at least 22 Blue Dog PAC-endorsed candidates winning Tuesday. Meanwhile, the Congressional Progressive Caucus anticipated at least 25 new members.

For House progressives, swing-seat wins in Michigan and California were signs that their candidates’ message resonated in battleground races and that Democrats should move to implement those progressive ideas next year.

“Big majorities can get big things done,” Congressional Progressive Caucus Chair Greg Casar said on a Wednesday press call.

Illinois Rep. Brad Schneider, the chair of the center-left New Democrat Coalition, told reporters he had a strong relationship with Casar and envisioned different factions of the party working together.

“It doesn’t matter what you call your idea. If your idea is going to help us move the ball forward, join us at the table,” Schneider said.

Army veteran Cait Conley, who was leading GOP Rep. Mike Lawler in a race that remained uncalled Wednesday afternoon, said she’d been open about having differences with incoming New York Democrats aligned with the Democratic Socialists of America. But she said Democrats across the political spectrum needed to prioritize “real solutions for the American people.”

“If we don’t make that our priority, if we don’t show that that is more important than anything else … then I think the voters are going to show us in 2028 that they’re not going to accept that,” she said on the New Dems press call.

While the president cast a shadow over the entire midterm map, three of his marquee candidates were among the night’s biggest busts.

Trump was behind appointed South Carolina Sen. Darline Graham every step of the way, helping her win a contested primary to replace her late brother, Lindsey Graham, on the ballot. But Graham lost to Democrat Annie Andrews in a stunning upset. In North Carolina, Trump had publicly urged Michael Whatley to run for the state’s open seat. But the former Republican National Committee chairman lost to Democratic former Gov. Roy Cooper by 20 points. And most significantly, in Texas, Trump elevated scandal-plagued state Attorney General Ken Paxton over Republican incumbent Sen. John Cornyn. But the president’s support wasn’t enough to stave off a general election drubbing at the hands of Democrat James Talarico.

Even Rep. Julia Letlow, Trump’s pick for Senate in deep-red Louisiana, won her election by less than 2 points.

Republicans in seven states successfully embarked on a mid-decade redistricting spree aimed at boosting GOP candidates. But the push couldn’t overcome the political headwinds that battered the Republican brand. In California, where Democrats launched a redistricting push in response to the Texas redraw, the new map proved more beneficial: Democrats are poised to flip at least four seats they redrew in their favor.

Republican Alex Mealer was the heavy favorite to win a redrawn Houston-area seat that Trump would have carried by 20 points. She raised $2.8 million to first-time Democratic candidate Leticia Gutierrez’ $215,000. Yet Gutierrez won the contest by double digits. In an interview on MS NOW, Gutierrez said her campaign proved that “you don’t need money to buy a congressional race.” She wasn’t the only under-the-radar House contender to pull off an unexpected victory Tuesday. In North Carolina, several low-profile and under-funded Democrats appear poised to pull off unlikely upsets, though the races remain too close to call.

Democrats have chased the dream of a blue Texas for more than 30 years — and always came up short. Until Tuesday. The party notched a series of stunning wins in the nation’s most consequential red state — from Talarico’s decisive victory in the Senate contest to winning several key House races to capturing the governor’s mansion. Democrats seized every elected statewide position on the ballot and flipped the state House, according to The Associated Press. “They said it could not be done,’’ Democratic Rep. Christian Menefee told supporters Tuesday night. “We did it.”

Democrats made deep gains in red states and districts across the country, flipping Senate seats in Texas, South Carolina and Kansas, while races in Alaska and Mississippi remained too close to call. Florida Gov. Ron DeSantis called it “probably the political worst case scenario for a midterm election.”

But Democrats didn’t appear convinced that their brand had completely rebounded from deep losses two years ago, saying they had work to do over the next two years.

“Many of the folks who voted for us yesterday, of course, are still skeptical,” Schumer said.

Similarly, Casar and Rep. Pramila Jayapal both warned Democrats not to assume that Tuesday’s wins had resolved the party’s branding issues.

“We still are not particularly popular as a Democratic Party across the country,” Jayapal told reporters. “I think we’ve got a shot to show that we can deliver on the kind of bold populist change that people ran on and won on.”

Meg’s Monster coming to PS5 on March 1, 2027

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Odencat will release a PlayStation 5 version of RPG adventure game Meg’s Monster on March 1, 2027, the companies announced.

Meg’s Monster first launched for Xbox One, Switch, and PC via Steam on March 2, 2023, followed by iOS and Android on May 7, 2026.

Additionally, the Meg’s Monster Complete Edition bundle containing the base game and downloadable content “Lost Memories” will be available for Switch on November 19. Both the downloadable content and bundle will be available for the PlayStation 5 version when it releases in 2027.

Here is an overview of the game, via Odencat:

Welcome to the Underworld—a land of monsters and mutants who eat humans for breakfast.

One such monster—a hulking ogre by the name of Roy—has no interest in eating humans. But one day, he and his best friend Golan stumble upon a lost little girl named Meg, and discover something truly terrifying: the moment she starts wailing in fear, a blood-red hue washes over the sky, and the earth itself begins to tremble and quake.

That’s right: this small girl’s tears hold the power to bring forth the apocalypse—and the only way to stop it is for them to help find her mother so she can make it safely back home.

Meg’s Monster is a short, story-driven JRPG with a big twist. Players control Roy, who begins the game with 99,999 HP and is virtually untouchable—but the real concern is keeping Meg safe, because if she starts crying, it’s game over for everyone. Players will have to master the unique mechanics and mini-games baked into each battle, all while protecting Meg and using her favorite toys to keep her pacified.

Over the course of their journey together, Roy and Meg will encounter a host of quirky and colorful characters, all lovingly rendered in Odencat’s trademark vibrant pixel aesthetic that makes even scary monsters look cute. There’s plenty of drama and intrigue to be found here—and even previously defeated enemies can be befriended through optional sub-events.

What begins as a relatively simple and heartwarming story about two unlikely friends ultimately expands into something much greater, as Roy and Meg slowly begin to unravel the mysteries surrounding the Underworld and themselves. The result is an emotional journey that, like any good storybook, will remain in your heart for years to come.

Park Hyatt Riviera Maya Cancun, Brand’s First All-Inclusive, Opens Soon

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Want to take advantage of Hyatt Privé benefits, including a room upgrade, complimentary breakfast, a hotel credit, and more, when booking a cash stay? Contact Ford ([email protected]) for more details. He may even be able to help if you already have a stay booked.

In late 2023, Hyatt and Parks Hospitality Holdings announced a collaboration that will expand Hyatt’s footprint in Mexico, with the development of four new properties. If you ask me, the most interesting of these is the new Park Hyatt Riviera Maya, which will also be the brand’s first all-inclusive property.

There’s now an update, as the hotel’s opening date has just been moved forward by a couple of months, and it’s now expected to open in the coming weeks.

The Park Hyatt Riviera Maya, located in Cancun, Mexico, is now accepting reservations for stays as of December 3, 2026. The hotel was initially supposed to open in 2025, then it started accepting reservations for stays as of February 2027, and now it’s accepting reservations for stays as of late 2026.

Hopefully the current opening date sticks, but if you book in the first several months the hotel is open, don’t be surprised if we see an additional delay, or at least don’t be surprised if not everything is ready.

The 148-key property will have a first-ever concept for the Park Hyatt brand, as it will be all-inclusive. Admittedly this is pretty normal for the Cancun area, but still, it’s noteworthy for a brand like Park Hyatt.

Hyatt has been leaning heavily into the all-inclusive space in recent times, particularly through acquisitions. So while Hyatt has grown massively in terms of its number of all-inclusive brands, it’s surprising to see even a traditionally non all-inclusive brand get this kind of an experience.

I’m generally not a fan of all-inclusives, or at least historically haven’t chosen to vacation at them. However, after my recent Ritz-Carlton Yacht Collection cruise, there is something I really enjoyed about not being nickel-and-dimed for everything, given how expensive luxury hospitality has become in so many places.

When it comes to the dining outlets, they’ll include Beach Grill IK’AL (the beachfront restaurant with seafood and Yucatecan flavors), Bruma (the steakhouse), Nutre Kitchen (the all-day dining restaurant), Pool Bar Brisa (the pool bar serving drinks & light snacks), Patisserie Market & Cafe (the coffee shop), Living Room (the lobby bar).

Accommodations will range in size from 559 square feet (52 square meters) to 2,303 square feet (214 square meters), so they’re generously sized, as you’d expect. The decor looks nice enough, but not like anything special.

As you’d expect, the resort will have amenities including several pools, a full-service spa, a gym, and more.

Once open, this adds to a growing number of luxury points hotels in the Cancun area. Just recently we saw the opening of the St. Regis Kanai and Waldorf Astoria Cancun, so it’s nice to see Hyatt’s most luxurious brand also expand to the Cancun area. As a matter of fact, the Park Hyatt will be immediately next to the Waldorf Astoria (which also recently became all-inclusive, as it wasn’t initially).

The Park Hyatt brand is really growing in Mexico right now. We’ve seen the recent opening of the Park Hyatt Los Cabos at Cabo Del Sol, while the Park Hyatt Mexico City is expected to open in early 2027. Beyond that, we also saw the recent opening of the Alila Mayakoba, another good luxury option in the Cancun area.

With the Park Hyatt Riviera Maya now bookable, what are rates like? If paying cash, rooms seem to generally start at around $1,500 per night, but that does include taxes & fees. So it’s obviously very expensive, but given Mexico’s high taxes & fees, it’s actually not quite as crazy as some may have expected.

Then again, I’m not necessarily expecting this to be typical Park Hyatt quality, given the extent to which this is outside the typical Park Hyatt wheelhouse. Oddly, at least as of now, rates seem to be the same whether one or two people are staying, which is unusual for an all-inclusive.

If you are looking to book a cash stay here, I’d recommend doing so through the Hyatt Privé program, which offers extra perks like room upgrades, a hotel credit, and more.

When redeeming points, the hotel follows World of Hyatt’s Category F all-inclusive award chart, which is the most expensive award pricing for all-inclusive properties.

Under Hyatt’s current award chart, the cost is typically 45,000 to 85,000 points per night, and that’s the same whether the room has single or double occupancy.

The Park Hyatt Riviera Maya is now accepting reservations for stays as of December 2026. The hotel is expected to be an all-inclusive, making it the first Park Hyatt property in the world where that’s the case.

It’s awesome to see Hyatt’s luxury expansion in Mexico, as this will be the third Park Hyatt to open in a fairly short time period. However, the Park Hyatt brand is becoming increasingly inconsistent, and I find the all-inclusive pivot to be a bit strange, especially given how many all-inclusive brands Hyatt already has.

What do you make of plans for the Park Hyatt Riviera Maya?

Zillow pushes Preferred agent program toward 100% of buyer connections

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Zillow reported Q3 2026 traffic down 3% to 215 million monthly users and a $158 million net loss. It highlighted AI mode expansion, Preferred growth and gains in mortgage and rentals revenue.  AI Summary

Zillow may be facing calls for investigations into some of its practices by consumer advocacy organizations and federal lawmakers, but the real estate listing portal giant is doubling down on its belief that it is “building the faster, easier and more affordable moving experience.”

“As more consumers and professionals use Zillow to move from dreaming to transacting, the old real estate experience will feel slow, difficult and expensive by comparison,”  Zillow CEO Jeremy Wacksman and Zillow COO and CFO Jeremy Hofmann wrote in a letter to shareholders published on Wednesday.

Despite this confidence, Zillow may be facing some headwinds. The company said that in the third quarter of 2026, visitor traffic to its apps and sites was down 3% during the quarter to 215 million average monthly unique users, while overall visits were down 5% annually to 2.3 billion according to Comscore data. Despite the declines, Comscore data shows that Zillow attracted 77% of average monthly unique visitors in the real estate category in Q3. In addition to this, the company said it has found that its AI search mode is deepening engagement, with visitors who use this option spending nearly three times as long as those who don’t on the site.

Zillow said that AI mode is now available to 100% of its signed-in users. The company said it was also exploring ways to use AI to make real estate professionals more productive and to help its internal engineers, who are using AI to ship 60% more changes to production than they did in 2024, according to Zillow.

“Buyers spend an average of two to three hours a week with us over about five months — and then for most, Zillow software and services power their transaction: the majority of residential real estate transactions in the U.S. run through our professional software,” the executives wrote. “Because of Zillow’s transaction infrastructure, our agentic AI does more than guide the shopping experience people love; it assists consumers and professionals throughout the transaction itself.

In addition to the decline in traffic, Zillow also reported a net loss of $158 million for the quarter, down from a $10 million net income generated in Q3 2025. This decline came even as revenue rose 7% annually to $724 million.

Zillow’s Preferred lead referral program is one of cornerstones of its revenue generation engine. The company reported that in Q3, two-thirds of buyer connections came to a Preferred agent partner in the integrated experience, roughly double what it was a year ago. Zillow said it was on track for Preferred to represent over three-quarters of connections by the end of the year, with it anticipating reaching 100% of connections in the first half of 2027. Zillow also noted that Preferred connections generate 35% more revenue per connection that its previous lead generation model.

Even with the growth of the Preferred program, Zillow’s for-sale revenue was flat year-over-year at $489 million. In addition to this, its residential segment recorded a 4% annual decline in revenue to $419 million, which the company attributed to higher-than-expected mortgage rates putting downward pressure on housing transaction volume.

Bright spots in Zillow’s Q3 earnings report occurred in its mortgage and rentals segments. Zillow’s mortgage rose 32% annually to $70 million, due to a 55% increase in purchase loan origination volume to $2.0 billion.

In markets where Zillow has integrated its mortgage product with its Preferred program the longest, Zillow said adoption rates of Zillow Home Loans is around 20%.

“We are also making Zillow Home Loans a trusted lender for Preferred agent partners’ entire book of business, not just for clients they connect with through Zillow,” Wacksman and Hofmann wrote.

Rentals revenue was up 26% year-over-year to $220 million, which the company attributed to multifamily revenue growing 35% year over year. Zillow said its rentals segment now accounts for roughly 30% of its total revenue, up from 21% two years ago. Additionally, the company noted that 81,000 multifamily properties were advertised on Zillow at the end of the third quarter, a 17% annual increase.

“Diversifying into this space has brought us a large growth opportunity and made our business more robust,” the executives wrote. “Our rentals strategy is to offer the broadest, most comprehensive inventory from single-family homes to large apartment buildings, and to streamline the transaction experience for consumers and housing providers.”

Executives added that they believe Zillow is still just scratching the surface of the opportunity presented by the rentals sector.

“We see a clear path to more than $1 billion in annual rentals revenue and a substantial opportunity to keep growing across both multifamily and longtail rentals well beyond that milestone,” Wacksman and Hofmann wrote.

The company also highlighted the performance of some of its other offerings, noting that its CRM product, Follow Up Boss, recorded roughly 141,000 monthly active users in Q3, up 16% annually. In addition to this, Zillow said over 800 brokerages had signed up for its pre-marketing product Zillow Preview and its listing enhancement product, Zillow Showcase, was on 5.4% of all new U.S. listings.

“Even as housing conditions deteriorated sharply, Zillow kept growing and outperforming, powered by our brand, audience, value to partners, disciplined cost management and strong growth levers across our well-diversified business,” Hofmann and Wacksman wrote. “These advantages are driving our performance through an exceptionally difficult macro environment today and position us to accelerate when conditions improve.”

Mexican Seafood and Natural Wines Are in the Spotlight at This New NYC Restaurant

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A stacked tuna tostada, puckering shrimp aguachile, and more seafood dishes stun at El Camarón Peludo

Known for their roasted and slow-cooked meats at Taqueria Ramirez, Tania Apolinar and Giovanni Cervantes partnered with chef Carlos Barrera to open a new seafood-focused wine bar this year. Here’s what a full day of prep looks like at El Camarón Peludo in the West Village.

First, fresh shrimp from Sinaloa, Mexico; Ecuador; and more locales are de-shelled and de-veined for three central shrimp dishes. Then, Cervantes starts the fresh salsa for the day, taste testing and comparing each batch to the salsa from last week to make sure the flavor is consistent. A bright red salsa is mixed with mayo to create the sauce on the camarones zarandeados, a shrimp version of a fish dish from the Pacific coast of Mexico that is usually grilled in a flat wire grill basket.

Upstairs, Apolinar prepares the wine bar for opening, laying out chilled seafood on ice in the window and wine bottles that will be placed in chilled buckets for service. She recommends sitting at the long, winding bar to watch the bartenders and chefs work.

Back in the kitchen, Barrera starts breaking down Spanish tuna for a creative tuna tostada layered with spicy mayo, fish eggs, habanero-lime oil, avocado, and crispy leeks. Then it’s taco prep, including the pescadilla (a deep-fried fish taco) and a shrimp taco with queso fresco, refried beans, and slices of roasted poblano peppers. Cervantes and Barrera prep the raw bar area, making salsa verde and salsa negro. The green salsa is made with parsley while the black salsa is layered with habaneros, piquin chile, and squid ink.

Cervantes explains that his original idea to pair the innovative seafood plates with bubbly wine turned into a natural wine bar, with El Camarón Peludo sourcing bottles from all over the world.
